# An Incredible Opportunity

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

**Basis: pattern.** An observation drawn from experience, offered as an observation.  
**Origin: author.** The author's own observation, from experience.  
**Theme: Coercion and control.**  
**Written for: Legislators and regulators · Law enforcement and investigators · Press and researchers · Households and individuals.**

**Not independently verified.**

**Series: Who answers for harm, part 7 of 7.** 1. [A regulator finding does not stop a deployment](IS_CON_findings-dont-stop-deployment.md) · 2. [Fined in Europe, hired in America](IS_CON_fined-in-europe-hired-in-america.md) · 3. [Have private contractors killed civilians and gone free?](IS_CON_contractors-killed-and-freed.md) · 4. [Zersetzung's methods are crimes](IS_CON_zersetzung-methods-are-crimes.md) · 5. [Who profits from a body?](IS_CON_who-profits-from-a-body.md) · 6. [Accountability isn't wired to deployment, even in the schema](IS_CON_accountability-not-wired.md) · 7. An Incredible Opportunity (this concept)

*This concept is the author's speculation. It does not assert that the system it describes exists. Every statement recorded in the journal is an external communication, and every statement heard on the bullhorn requires verification. Read this concept under the [Critical Disclaimer](/disclaimer).*

---

## The opportunity

There is an opportunity, right now, for any organization to come forward with evidence of what is described across this archive. If it is happening, could it be considered a [crime against humanity](/glossary/crimes-against-humanity)? An atrocity? A war crime?

Statements have suggested that what people are being subjected to is, among other things, a *risk-mitigation system*. Historically, it has been suggested, [Zersetzung](/glossary/zersetzung-tactics) tactics, the East German method of "disintegration," are used to resolve what is seen as an insider threat: a person who cannot be reached through a relationship, including a paid one.

*On the record:* Zersetzung's methods are crimes in the United States, and a court has punished them. In August 2019, members of eBay's security team ran a harassment campaign against a Massachusetts couple who published a newsletter critical of the company. Seven former employees were convicted, and eBay paid a $3 million criminal penalty. In July 2026, almost seven years after the campaign began, the couple's civil suit settled for a reported $55.7 million (see [Zersetzung's methods are crimes](/concepts/zersetzung-methods-are-crimes)).

## Who could come forward first?

So what is the opportunity? The first the author would suggest: the Trump administration could release information on what is happening to the general population of the world.

In the author's speculation, the first municipality, federal agency or country to come forward with this information would be remembered as the saviour of the global economy, and of humanity. That view rests on statements and on human experience, which are qualitative and unverified.

Or it could be a city. Denver, Colorado, is home to the Denver Federal Center, the largest concentration of federal agencies outside Washington, D.C. Denver could come forward with what is happening.

## What a first warning could look like

Authorized state and local agencies can already send a Wireless Emergency Alert to every phone in an area. It arrives with a loud alert tone. At the bare minimum, a message could reach everyone at once.

A hypothetical example, written by the author. No such alert has ever been issued:

> **EMERGENCY ALERT**
>
> Life-threatening long-range acoustic device (LRAD) technology has been detected in your area. Do not let anyone into your home. Do not go outside looking for anyone, under any circumstances. An investigation has begun. Follow the news media for further explanation.

*On the record:* long-range acoustic devices are real, commercially sold and owned by American police departments (see [Are Denver citizens subject to acoustic weapons?](/concepts/denver-acoustic-weapons)). Nothing on record shows one being used in the way this archive describes.

## Are people leaving their homes?

From a municipality's point of view, here are a few questions.

Are people abandoning their homes? Moving into trailers? Travelling from city to city, looking for one that is not reached by the [neurotech bullhorn](/concepts/the-neurotech-bullhorn), and where they are not hunted in their own homes by Zersetzung tactics?

## The city that says it first

Hypothetically, suppose a city or state, whether Denver, Colorado; Portland, Oregon; Seattle, Washington; Los Angeles and Southern California; or Atlanta, Georgia, held a press conference and said: *We are banning Zersetzung tactics and euthanization. We are making arrests. We have active indictments. We are bringing in the people who supported this experimentation and opened the way to a Trojan horse attack.*

What would follow? Would people around the world race to that city? Would they move there immediately? Would they start investing there, because it had named the technology and the attack out loud?

A sample message, written by the author:

> **Denver, Colorado has officially become one of the safest cities in the world. The reasons why may surprise you.**

*On the record:* a city does not need a new law to act. Stalking, harassment and intimidation are already crimes under Colorado law and federal law, and conspiring to intimidate someone out of their rights is a federal crime (see [Zersetzung's methods are crimes](/concepts/zersetzung-methods-are-crimes)). What a city can add is enforcement, and an explanation.

## Is the whole neurotech industry bottlenecked?

What does the opportunity actually look like? Start with what is already real.

*On the record:* brain-computer interfaces have left the laboratory, and the first products are aimed at healthcare. In January 2024 Neuralink implanted its first human participant with its device, called Telepathy, which lets a person with paralysis control a computer by thought. Its VOICE trial is testing thought-to-speech for people who cannot speak, including a participant with ALS. Its Blindsight implant, meant to restore some vision, received an FDA Breakthrough Device designation in September 2024. These are medical devices, implanted with consent, in clinical trials (see [Can a machine read what you are thinking?](/concepts/can-a-machine-read-thought)).

Now set them beside what is hypothesized elsewhere on this site, such as automatic language translation ([The Diving Ecosystem](/concepts/diving-ecosystem), Capability 4). If it could be done with consent, a person who cannot speak, in any language, could be understood at once. A traveller overseas could ask for a cab, or for food, simply by looking at someone and communicating, and be understood. No shared language. No translation app. It would just work. It would seem like a miracle. But it must be consented to.

People with ALS, losing the ability to move and to speak, could control their devices and be heard. That is part of the opportunity too. In the author's view it is out there right now, but it is not being used or favoured. Instead, according to human experience and to statements on the neurotech bullhorn, communicating is still treated, as of October 2026, as an unofficial death sentence (see [Do Not Choose Yourself](/concepts/do-not-choose-yourself)).

So the opportunity is there. What is taking so long?

## What unblocking it could mean

**Jobs.** Unblocking the neurotech market could create jobs in education, in healthcare, in the military and across the technology industry. Any industry would benefit from a method of communication like this.

**Public safety.** Imagine a mobile app that tells you who is observing you, and who is communicating with you.

**Accountability.** Suppose, hypothetically, that the Department of Homeland Security were protecting people through this method of communication, should it choose to. How would anyone thank or reward it, if no one can tell that it is Homeland Security? There is no badge number, and no way to leave a public review.

In the author's view, using the modern telephone and the neurotech bullhorn as a weapon is the least valuable use of this technology that could ever be imagined.

*If anyone tries to get you to open your door or come outside, do not go. In an emergency, call 911. If you are struggling, call or text 988.*

## What this does not answer

- If what is described in this archive is happening, could it meet the definition of a crime against humanity: acts such as murder, torture and persecution, committed as part of a widespread or systematic attack directed against a civilian population?
- Is the experience described here a "risk-mitigation system," as statements have suggested? If so, whose risk is being mitigated, and at whose expense?
- If Zersetzung tactics are used on people who cannot be reached through a relationship, what happens to everyone else around them?
- Why did it take almost seven years for the couple harassed by eBay's security team to reach a civil settlement? How long would a case like that take if the harassment were telepathic?
- Who would be first to come forward: a federal administration, a state, a city, or another country?
- Could a Wireless Emergency Alert be used to warn a whole city at once? Who would decide to send it?
- Are people abandoning their homes, or moving from city to city, to escape the bullhorn?
- If one city announced arrests and indictments, would people move there? Would they invest there?
- If being first is such an opportunity, why has no one taken it?
- Is the whole neurotech industry bottlenecked? If consented telepathic communication is possible, who is holding it back, and why?
- If automatic translation worked mind to mind, with consent, what would it mean for people who cannot speak, in any language?
- What would an app that shows who is observing you, and who is communicating with you, change for public safety?
- How can anyone reward, or hold to account, an agency they cannot identify: no badge number, no public review?


## Author's note

**Being first matters.** The author believes the first organization to come forward will be remembered for it. Coming second will look like following.

**An explanation is the protection.** A warning on every phone, even a few lines long, would do more to protect people than anything else, because it would tell them this is not the spirit world, and not something that cannot be stopped.

**The safest city.** The city that names this out loud, enforces the laws it already has, and explains what is happening could become one of the safest places in the world to live.

**The least value.** Using the telephone and the bullhorn as a weapon is the least value this technology could ever deliver. The same channel, with consent, could give a voice to people who have none, in any language.

**Consent first.** Every part of this opportunity depends on consent. Without it, none of it is an opportunity.

*Commentary by the author. Not evidence, and not a finding of this research.*

- [2. Invisible Ships — Are Denver citizens subject to acoustic weapons?](/concepts/denver-acoustic-weapons)

The last is a crisis line, listed for readers' safety, not as evidence. None of them shows that the system this concept describes exists.*


===== FILE: concepts/IS_CON_whose-eyesight-is-it.md · ID: IS-CON-WHOSE-EYESIGHT-IS-IT =====

# Why does the camera on your door not answer to you?

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

**Basis: structural.** This follows from what the dataset does or does not contain.  
**Origin: author.** The author's own observation, from experience.  
**Theme: Surveillance and the person.**  
**Written for: Households and individuals · Legislators and regulators.**

**Not independently verified.**

**Series: Surveillance in everyday life, part 1 of 7.** 1. Why does the camera on your door not answer to you? (this concept) · 2. [Who could refuse a system that saved their child?](IS_CON_prevention-as-the-product.md) · 3. [Does being watched change what people let themselves think?](IS_CON_no-private-thinking-space.md) · 4. [How protected is your medical information?](IS_CON_how-protected-is-your-medical-record.md) · 5. [What are children subject to?](IS_CON_what-children-are-subject-to.md) · 6. [What happens to everyone around a target?](IS_CON_everyone-around-a-target.md) · 7. [Are people made into intelligence assets without knowing it?](IS_CON_made-into-assets-unknowing.md)

A doorbell camera watches your street and answers to its manufacturer. You can look at what it recorded; you do not hold it, and you cannot say who else can. The proposal here is narrower than a privacy argument and harder to dismiss: give a citizen their own eyesight — one recording that belongs to the person the way a body camera belongs to an officer.

The procurement record gives that proposal an uncomfortable shape. Amazon Web Services is the largest government cloud vendor in this register by a wide margin: $42.9bn across 27 awards, and 93 deployments in 21 of the 35 geographies tracked. Its GovCloud capability list includes Amazon Rekognition — face detection and comparison across image and video. The company that sells the doorbell also sells the platform.

That is a statement about concentration, not a connection between the two, and nothing in this record shows one. What it does show is who has a column. Across 1,922 procurement entries, ten describe an individual. All ten are litigants.

## Evidence

- AWS: $42.9bn across 27 awards; 93 deployments across 21 of the 35 geographies in the register
- Amazon Rekognition — face detection/comparison and image/video analysis — listed under AWS GovCloud (US)
- 10 of 1,922 procurement records describe an individual; every one of them is a litigant
- Ring appears nowhere in this register: the consumer product sits outside the procurement record entirely

## What this does not answer

- Who would hold a citizen-owned recording, and on whose infrastructure?
- Is a record you own but cannot take off the platform really yours?
- What would it take for the observed person to appear in a procurement register at all?

## References

- [There is no column for you](/concepts#no-column-for-you)
- [Who profits from a body?](/concepts#who-profits-from-a-body)

*Both describe the gap this proposal is aimed at. Neither shows that a consumer camera and a government platform are linked.*

# Why Hasn't It Been Turned Off?

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

**Basis: pattern.** An observation drawn from experience, offered as an observation.  
**Origin: author.** The author's own observation, from experience.  
**Theme: Neurotechnology.**  
**Written for: Households and individuals · Law enforcement and investigators · Legislators and regulators · Press and researchers.**

**Not independently verified.**

**Series: Telepathic communication, part 3 of 3.** 1. [The Diving Ecosystem](IS_CON_diving-ecosystem.md) · 2. [Diving](IS_CON_diving.md) · 3. Why Hasn't It Been Turned Off? (this concept)

If this experience is the work of a military deployment, then it is the work of a machine operated by human beings. And a machine can be turned off at any time. So why hasn't it been?

*This concept is the author's speculation. It does not assert that the system it describes exists. Every statement recorded in the journal is an external communication, and every statement heard on the bullhorn, like the bullhorn itself, requires verification. Read this concept under the [Critical Disclaimer](/disclaimer).*

---

## Two tragic ironies

**The first is the story people are told:** that this is the spirit world, or something extraterrestrial; that no one can stop it; that there is no hope. If it is a machine operated by human beings, none of that is true.

**The second is the simplest.** The surest way never to "euthanize" anyone for communicating is not to harass them in the first place. The offices doing this can stop at any time. They can simply choose not to communicate. No law of nature compels any of it. This is the work of human beings, and no one has to communicate with anyone.

## Who gains from harassment?

Doesn't law enforcement lose every covert opportunity the moment harassment begins? A person who is being harassed knows they are being watched, and a person who knows they are being watched is no longer a covert source of anything. The author is not an intelligence analyst, but the logic seems plain.

And if someone has been experimented on through this system, isn't the best thing anyone could do for them to stop, immediately? What would be surreal, and unusually cruel, is the opposite: [Zersetzung](/glossary/zersetzung-tactics) tactics used to force a person to accept a home invasion, a beheading, a dissection.

## How it began, for the author

According to statements the author has received, he was observed with similar technology from as early as 1998. In all that time he was never spoken to and never harassed, except on rare occasions, and on those occasions what he heard was supportive.

Looking back, the first event that was different from anything before came within weeks of his first COVID-19 vaccination, with Moderna: a silent observer, a brain-to-brain connection. A Pfizer booster followed.

Then, in Portland, Oregon, after his divorce, came something the author can only describe as a horror film. There was no verbal communication at all: only [phantom sensations](/glossary/phantom-sensations) and internal mental images.

When he returned to Denver, the conversations began, with people introducing themselves as the spirit world or as extraterrestrials. As a research professional he was always skeptical, but for a long time he had no explanation.

And then the violence began; the author calls it ultraviolence. Verbal harassment. Attempts to gain entry to his home, or to lure him outside. Shouting, with everyone listening: "Put your hand up for assistance." "Put your motherfucking hand up." Public outcry at three in the morning. Ultrasonic sound attacks, [tinnitus](/glossary/tinnitus), and relentless harassment.

There is no proof that the vaccination has anything to do with this experience, and the author does not claim it does. It is simply the one event that may have affected his health, and the timing is what he observed. Statements on the bullhorn have said, again and again, that no implant is needed to experience [voice-to-skull](/glossary/voice-to-skull) (V2K) communication.

*On the record:* the ingredients of the COVID-19 vaccines are published, and none of them is a device, a sensor or anything that could receive or transmit a signal. That two things happen close together in time does not mean one caused the other (see [Next to each other is not because of each other](/concepts/co-occurrence-is-not-cause)).

## Has it been turned off before?

Based on past statements, and on the author's own experience, the machine has been turned off more than once, and each time the author felt a significant drop, as if his body's chemistry had fallen away.

Statements on the bullhorn, and the author's experience, suggest that anyone connected to this system may feel a rise in serotonin and dopamine simply from being connected to others who are also connected. If so, what happens when it is switched off abruptly? It has been suggested that it should not be: given the ultrasonic sound attacks and the tinnitus people may be living with, the system should be tapered off rather than cut, because of its effect on the human brain.

*On the record:* no published research documents a remote system that raises serotonin or dopamine. What is documented is that social connection affects mood, and that abrupt changes in stimulation, sleep or stress can make people feel worse before they feel better. Anyone feeling a sudden drop in mood should talk to a doctor; if you are struggling, you can call or text 988 at any time.

## The flip side: what if it simply stopped?

How safe would you feel if you were living with this, and out of nowhere it stopped?

Statements have suggested that the author has a degree of protection. When the system goes down, he does not feel better; he feels worse. At the moment, the observation feels like protection, from at least one part of those observing. If the system were turned off, would mayhem follow? Would the people carrying out the harassment and the Zersetzung tactics, and pressing for euthanization, take that cover of darkness to enter people's homes?

Without an explanation, everything feels like a terrible vulnerability, even when the system is off. That is the author's personal opinion. Turning it off is not enough on its own. It has to be explained.

## What has been said about why

Over time, statements have offered reasons for leaving it running.

**"We do their bidding."** One, in paraphrase: *We need to get to know the people speaking with us. We do their bidding, in an attempt to understand what they want.* If that is the reasoning, the author asks, what does it mean for everyone else? While someone studies the people giving the orders, who is studying what the orders do to the people living under them?

**"There is an investigation."** Another, repeated historically: it cannot be turned off because there is an investigation; the people responsible have to be caught and identified, and that need is being weighed against keeping things covert. But here is the tragic irony again. Because of the bullhorn, and because of how horrifying a first experience of telepathic communication is, there is nothing covert about what the general population is living through.

**A campaign, in public.** Consider what it means to run a campaign against a journalist, or against anyone from any country, and to keep it going on the bullhorn with statements like these. Attempts to gain permission to enter someone's home: *"Put your hand up. Put your motherfucking hand up for assistance."* Another large pattern: *"Open your door for the spirit world."* If a person puts their hand up, does someone record that as legally binding consent to euthanasia? If someone then comes to the door and is let in, what happens? In the author's view, that person loses their life. Do not raise your hand, do not open the door, and call 911.

It is the predatory nature of all this, intensely predatory, that the author cannot reconcile with anyone trying to prolong a relationship to learn what an enemy wants. It feels less like a strategy than a reaction.

**"It's always been this way" — or "it never existed."** Asked why anyone would try to gain entry to a home, a very large pattern of statements has answered, in paraphrase: *It's an industry standard. It's always been this way. This is just how it's done.* Lately, as of October 2026, the suggestion has been the opposite: *This never existed. It's a new process.* Both cannot be true. Which is it?

## Where is the news media?

Why is there no explanation in the news? The record includes another large pattern of communication, which may not appear in the downloadable transcripts: that the news media industry, along with other technology organizations, has been given access to government cloud systems, and the opportunity to experience the eyesight system.

As part of that system, statements describe a risk-mitigation layer. In paraphrase: *We have to include euthanization. We have to include it.* As if healthcare, and an explanation, were not options. Other statements, again in paraphrase: *Historically, we protect national security.* But as a citizen, the author was never harassed before his vaccination.

Another statement goes further. In paraphrase: *Euthanization is in the building.* The suggestion is that news offices have a "euthanization therapist," a psychologist or mental-health professional, on site. Is there any truth to this? And if there were, how would it affect the people who work there, knowing that someone in the building was there for that purpose?

These are reported statements, not findings. Is any of it true? And if it is, who decided that silence was the safer course? (See [Why isn't any of this in the news?](/concepts/why-isnt-this-in-the-news))

## Is anyone in government being told?

Are local and federal leaders being harassed right alongside everyone else, without an explanation? Are we paralyzed as a nation?

Statements also describe local law enforcement taking part in the Diving process, and being remarkably receptive to any education or speculation that might give them an advantage.

Historical statements go further: that the United States, the People's Republic of China and the Russian Federation are each divided down the middle, and that what is being experienced is the harm done by a union of private organizations, representing neither the United States nor the Communist Party. Is that so? And if it is, what is the explanation?

## Why 2027?

For two to three years, statements on the bullhorn have pointed to 2027 as a year of significance. One, in paraphrase: *I think the window for unconsented research has closed early.*

Set that beside the law on ending a life. In most of the world, killing a person, even at their request, is a serious crime; only a handful of countries and states allow assisted dying, and only under strict conditions. Jack Kevorkian, who helped more than a hundred people die, was convicted of second-degree murder in Michigan in 1999 and served eight years in prison.

In Colorado, where the author lives, the End-of-Life Options Act allows [medical aid in dying](/glossary/self-elected-suicide) only for an adult with a terminal illness, a prognosis of six months or less, and the capacity to decide for themselves.

Canada's law is broader. Since 2021 a person need not be terminally ill: a serious and incurable illness, disease or disability, in an advanced state of decline, with intolerable suffering, can qualify. But where a mental illness is the *only* condition, eligibility has been paused, twice, and is now set to begin on **17 March 2027**. A bill introduced in 2025 would make that pause permanent. Financial hardship has never been a ground for eligibility under Canadian law, although critics have raised concerns that poverty and a lack of support may lie behind some requests.

That is a documented fact, and it sits alongside the bullhorn statements. When the author asked about it, the answer, in paraphrase, was that 2027 marks the end of unconsented research, and that Canada is protecting its citizens until then from choosing to end their lives unnecessarily.

Is that the connection? And if something is meant to end in 2027, why wait for it?

## A machine, not a spirit

This experience is the outcome of a machine. A machine can be turned off, and, if what the author describes is true, it has been before. So why is it still running? And when it is turned off, who will explain what it was?

Remember, all of this is hypothetical. Every statement heard on the bullhorn, and the bullhorn itself, requires verification.

*Related glossary: [Zersetzung tactics](/glossary/zersetzung-tactics) · [Phantom sensations](/glossary/phantom-sensations) · [Tinnitus](/glossary/tinnitus) · [Telepathy](/glossary/telepathy) · [Diving](/glossary/diving) · [Voice-to-skull (V2K)](/glossary/voice-to-skull) · [The Mosquito](/glossary/mosquito-device) · [Targeted individual](/glossary/targeted-individual) · [Self-elected suicide](/glossary/self-elected-suicide)*

*Related concepts: [The Diving Ecosystem](/concepts/diving-ecosystem) · [Diving](/concepts/diving) · [What is the neurotech bullhorn?](/concepts/the-neurotech-bullhorn) · [Why isn't any of this in the news?](/concepts/why-isnt-this-in-the-news) · [Your house is not haunted](/concepts/what-produces-the-feeling) · [Zersetzung's methods are crimes](/concepts/zersetzung-methods-are-crimes) · [Next to each other is not because of each other](/concepts/co-occurrence-is-not-cause)*

## What this does not answer

- If this is a machine operated by human beings, who has the authority to turn it off?
- Why would anyone be punished for communicating, when the people operating the system could simply stop communicating first?
- Does harassment destroy the covert value of the very surveillance it relies on?
- If it has been switched off before, why was it switched back on?
- If switching it off abruptly harms people, what would a safe, tapered shutdown look like, and who would be responsible for it?
- If it were switched off without an explanation, would people be safer, or more exposed?
- Who benefits from people believing it is the spirit world, or extraterrestrial, and cannot be stopped?
- If the author was observed for years without being harassed, what changed, and who decided it?
- Has the news media been given access to this system, as statements suggest? If so, why hasn't it explained it?
- Are local and federal leaders living with this too, without an explanation?
- If a union of private organizations, representing no nation, is behind the harm, as statements suggest, who holds it to account?
- If the system is kept running to learn what its operators want, as statements suggest, what is it costing everyone else in the meantime?
- If an investigation is the reason it keeps running, what is left to keep covert, when the general population can hear it?
- Does anyone treat a raised hand, or an opened door, as consent to euthanasia? Under what law?
- Is this an "industry standard" that has always existed, or a "new process" that never did? Why has the answer changed?
- Why do statements on the bullhorn point to 2027, and why does that match the date Canada set for assisted dying where mental illness is the only condition?
- If unconsented research is meant to end in 2027, who decided the date, and why should anyone wait for it?
- Is there any truth to the statement that news offices have a "euthanization therapist" in the building? If so, what would that do to the people who work there?


## Author's note

**There is hope.** The belief that this cannot be stopped is part of what keeps it going. A machine can be turned off.

**Why wait?** The author does not expect anything to change in 2027, and he thinks waiting for it to find out is absurd. If it can be turned off, it can be turned off now.

**Turning it off is not enough.** Without an explanation, even silence feels like danger. Shutting the system down has to come with telling people what it was.

*Commentary by the author. Not evidence, and not a finding of this research.*

# Why isn't any of this in the news?

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

**Basis: documented.** A source, ruling or official record supports this directly.  
**Origin: ai.** Derived by AI analysis of the dataset.  
**Theme: The record and its limits.**  
**Written for: Press and researchers.**

**Not independently verified.**

**Series: How to read this archive, part 7 of 8.** 1. [Next to each other is not because of each other](IS_CON_co-occurrence-is-not-cause.md) · 2. [We cannot prove which came first, the law or the system](IS_CON_sequence-cannot-be-proven.md) · 3. [“Official” is not the same as “independent”](IS_CON_official-is-not-independent.md) · 4. [The headline spending figure is not what governments spent](IS_CON_headline-spending-is-not-spending.md) · 5. [The numbers under the numbers](IS_CON_low-number-may-mean-low-counting.md) · 6. [Prescribing is not a measure of illness](IS_CON_prescribing-is-not-prevalence.md) · 7. Why isn't any of this in the news? (this concept) · 8. [There is no column for you](IS_CON_no-column-for-you.md)

The usual assumption is that silence means suppression. There is a duller explanation with far better evidence behind it, and anyone reasoning about an absence of coverage should meet it first. Local journalism in the United States has collapsed. Northwestern's Medill School has tracked it annually; its 2025 State of Local News report counts nearly 3,500 newspapers gone since 2005 — close to forty per cent of all local papers in the country — with 136 lost in the last year alone. Two hundred and thirteen counties now have no local news source of any kind. A further 1,524 counties have exactly one, usually a weekly. Roughly fifty million Americans live with limited or no access to local news. More than 270,000 newspaper jobs have disappeared since 2005, a decline of over seventy-five per cent. So for a large part of the country, the question is not why reporters did not cover something. It is that there is no reporter. No one attends the council meeting, reads the court docket, or files the records request. Things do not go uncovered because they were buried; they go uncovered because the institution that used to notice them was dissolved for economic reasons over two decades, in public, with the numbers published every year. An absence of coverage is therefore very weak evidence of anything. It was weak evidence before any particular story existed.

## Evidence

- Medill State of Local News report, 2025: nearly 3,500 newspapers lost since 2005, close to 40% of all US local papers
- 136 newspapers closed or merged in the last year alone
- 213 counties have no local news source at all; nearly 80% of them predominantly rural
- 1,524 further counties have only one outlet, usually a weekly
- ~50 million Americans live with limited or no access to local news
- 270,000+ newspaper jobs lost since 2005, a decline exceeding 75%

## What this does not answer

- This explains why coverage is absent in general. It does not prove that no specific story was ever suppressed, and it is not offered as proof of that.
- National outlets still exist. The collapse documented here is local, and the concept does not extend to national editorial decisions.
- That an absence has an ordinary explanation does not establish that nothing happened. It establishes that the absence itself carries almost no information.

## References

- [Medill State of Local News report — Nieman Lab summary (2025)](https://www.niemanlab.org/2025/10/in-medills-latest-state-of-local-news-report-a-festering-20-year-old-problem-looms-larger-than-ever/)
- [What are children subject to?](/concepts#what-children-are-subject-to)
- [Next to each other is not because of each other](/concepts#co-occurrence-is-not-cause)

*Medill is cited for its own annual count. The last link is method: this concept is about what an absence can and cannot support, which is the same discipline applied to a different kind of gap.*

## Scope

This concept reports published research on the decline of local news. It makes no claim about the editorial decisions of any outlet, and does not establish that any particular story was or was not suppressed.

[Accountability isn't wired to deployment, even in the schema](IS_CON_accountability-not-wired.md) · 7. [An Incredible Opportunity](IS_CON_who-will-say-it-first.md)

No law names Zersetzung, but its methods are crimes, and a US court has punished them. In August 2019 members of eBay's security team ran a campaign against Ina and David Steiner, who published a newsletter in Natick, Massachusetts, that was critical of the company. They sent live insects, a bloody pig mask, a funeral wreath and a book on surviving a spouse's death. They posted the couple's address with invitations to strangers, sent threats under invented identities, followed them, and planned to break into their garage to put a tracker on their car. Then they deleted evidence and lied to police.

Seven former employees were convicted; the security director was sentenced to 57 months in prison. eBay admitted the facts in a deferred prosecution agreement and paid a $3 million criminal penalty, the statutory maximum, under three years of independent monitoring. In July 2026 the couple's civil suit settled for a reported $55.7 million.

The charges map onto Zersetzung's methods: stalking through interstate travel and electronic means (18 U.S.C. § 2261A), conspiracy (§ 371), witness tampering (§ 1512) and destroying records (§ 1519). The same conduct is criminal harassment and stalking under Massachusetts law, and stalking and harassment in Colorado. When two or more people conspire to intimidate someone out of their rights, federal law (§ 241) applies.

Germany never made Zersetzung itself a crime after 1990, because laws cannot punish acts retroactively. It lets victims be formally rehabilitated instead, and since 2019 anyone recognised as the target of a Zersetzung measure receives a one-off payment of €1,500.

## Evidence

- August 2019, Natick, Massachusetts: a campaign by members of eBay's security team against the publishers of a newsletter critical of the company, set out in federal charging documents
- Seven former employees convicted; the security director sentenced on 29 September 2022 to 57 months in prison, the last defendant sentenced on 18 July 2024
- Charged under 18 U.S.C. § 371 (conspiracy), § 2261A (stalking by interstate travel and by facilities of commerce), § 1512(b)(3) (witness tampering) and § 1519 (destroying or falsifying records)
- 11 January 2024: eBay entered a deferred prosecution agreement, admitted the facts, paid a $3 million criminal penalty and accepted a three-year independent compliance monitor
- 27 July 2026: the couple's civil suit (D. Mass., No. 1:21-cv-11181) settled for a reported $55.7 million
- Massachusetts: criminal harassment, M.G.L. c. 265 § 43A; stalking, c. 265 § 43. Colorado: stalking, C.R.S. 18-3-602; harassment, C.R.S. 18-9-111
- Germany: Zersetzung was not prosecuted as such after 1990; the 1994 rehabilitation laws and a 2019 amendment provide rehabilitation and a one-off €1,500 payment for targets of Zersetzung measures

## What this does not answer

- How would a target show a coordinated campaign when no single act looks criminal on its own?
- Who investigates when the conduct crosses state or national lines?
- This concept reports one prosecuted case. It does not claim that any other campaign described in this archive has been proven.

## References

- [Two former eBay executives sentenced to prison for cyberstalking — US Attorney, District of Massachusetts](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ma/pr/two-former-ebay-executives-sentenced-prison-cyberstalking)
- [eBay to pay $3 million in connection with corporate cyberstalking campaign — US Attorney, District of Massachusetts](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ma/pr/ebay-inc-pay-3-million-connection-corporate-cyberstalking-campaign-targeting)
- [Final defendant in eBay cyberstalking case sentenced — US Attorney, District of Massachusetts](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ma/pr/final-defendant-ebay-cyberstalking-case-sentenced)
- [Settlement of the civil suit — The Boston Globe, 27 July 2026](https://www.bostonglobe.com/2026/07/27/business/ebay-harassed-ina-david-steiner-settlement/)
- [18 U.S.C. § 2261A, stalking — Cornell Legal Information Institute](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/2261A)
- [18 U.S.C. § 241, conspiracy against rights — Cornell Legal Information Institute](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/241)
- [Massachusetts General Laws c. 265 § 43A, criminal harassment](https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartIV/TitleI/Chapter265/Section43A)
- [Massachusetts General Laws c. 265 § 43, stalking](https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartIV/TitleI/Chapter265/Section43)
- [Colorado Revised Statutes 18-3-602, stalking](https://colorado.public.law/statutes/crs_18-3-602)
- [Colorado Revised Statutes 18-9-111, harassment](https://colorado.public.law/statutes/crs_18-9-111)
- [Rehabilitation laws for victims of the SED dictatorship — Bundesstiftung zur Aufarbeitung der SED-Diktatur](https://www.bundesstiftung-aufarbeitung.de/de/erinnern/opfer-und-betroffene/juristische-aufarbeitung/rehabilitierungsgesetze)
- [Federal Administrative Court on the Zersetzung payment, 14 December 2023](https://www.bverwg.de/pm/2023/93)
- [Organised covert harassment of individuals is established fact](/concepts/organised-harassment-is-fact)

*The Department of Justice releases and charging documents are cited for the conduct, charges and sentences; the Boston Globe for the civil settlement; the statutes for what the law prohibits; the German sources for how Germany has treated Zersetzung since 1990. The linked concept records the wider set of adjudicated harassment cases.*

## Scope

This concept reports an adjudicated case and the laws that apply to its conduct. It makes no claim that any other organisation or person has committed any crime.

## brain–computer interface (BCI)

: A brain–computer interface (BCI), sometimes called a brain–machine interface (BMI), is a direct communication link between the brain's electrical activity and an external device, most commonly a computer or robotic limb. BCIs are often directed at researching, mapping, assisting, augmenting, or repairing human cognitive or sensory-motor functions. They are often conceptualized as a human–machine interface that skips the intermediary of moving body parts (e.g. hands or feet). BCI implementations range from non-invasive (EEG, MEG, MRI) and partially invasive (ECoG and endovascular) to invasive (microelectrode array), based on how physically close electrodes are to brain tissue.

*—*[*Wikipedia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain%E2%80%93computer_interface#:~:text=A%20brain%E2%80%93computer%20interface%20(BCI,(e.g.%20hands%20or%20feet).)

## chilling effect

The chilling effect is when individuals or groups are discouraged from exercising their legal or natural rights, such as freedom of speech, due to the fear of legal, social, or economic consequences. This can be caused by laws, regulations, or even private actions that are overly broad, vague, or inconsistent, leading people to self-censor to avoid potential punishment or retaliation.

Impact on Free Speech In the context of the First Amendment in the U.S., the chilling effect is a significant concern. It undermines the "marketplace of ideas" by stifling open discourse and debate, particularly on controversial or unpopular topics. It makes it harder for the public to hold government officials and powerful institutions accountable.

*—Google Gemini Summary*

```yaml
author: Sean C. Harris

disclaimer: meta/IS_META_terms.md
```

## cognitive liberty

Cognitive liberty is an evolving concept that asserts the fundamental right of an individual to have sovereignty over their own mind and consciousness. It goes beyond the traditional right to "freedom of thought" by addressing the new challenges posed by advances in neuroscience and technology.

The rise of technologies that directly interact with the human brain is making cognitive liberty a critical issue. These technologies include:

  - Brain-Computer Interfaces (BCIs): Devices that can read brain signals (e.g., to control a prosthetic limb) or send signals to the brain (e.g., to treat depression).
  - Neuro-enhancements: Technologies and drugs designed to improve memory, concentration, or other cognitive functions.
  - Artificial Intelligence (AI): AI algorithms and generative AI can be used to exploit cognitive biases, micro-target individuals with persuasive content, and even reconstruct thoughts from brain data, raising concerns about mental manipulation and privacy.

These developments pose a number of ethical and legal dilemmas. For example, if a company can monitor an employee's brain activity to measure productivity, is that a violation of cognitive liberty? Should the government be able to mandate "cognitive enhancement" for certain jobs?

Ultimately, cognitive liberty seeks to protect the individual's right to control their most private and intimate sphere—their mind. It is seen as a necessary foundation for all other human rights, as the ability to think freely is a prerequisite for freedom of speech, belief, and action.

  

*—Google Gemini Summary*

## perceptual set

hard·​ware,    HARD-wair,   *noun*

A Perceptual Set is a tendency to perceive or notice some aspects of the available sensory data and ignore others. It is heavily influenced by expectations, past experience and context. If an object is completely outside a person's prior experience (their "set"), they may not select it for conscious attention, effectively rendering it "invisible" to their mind's eye, even if their retinas physically register it.

## software-as-a-service (SaaS)

: Software as a Service (SaaS) is a cloud-based software delivery model where users access applications over the internet, typically through a web browser, instead of installing and managing them locally. The cloud provider manages the underlying infrastructure, security, maintenance, and updates. SaaS is accessed on a subscription basis, meaning users pay a fee for access to the software.

## terrorism

ter·ror·ism,   TAIR-uh-riz-uhm,   *noun*

: the systematic use of terror especially as a means of coercion **Merriam Webster Dictionary*

: violent, criminal acts committed by individuals and/or groups to further ideological goals stemming from domestic influences, such as those of a political, religious, social, racial, or environmental nature **FBI, domestic terrorism*

: violent, criminal acts committed by individuals and/or groups who are inspired by, or associated with, designated foreign terrorist organizations or nations (state-sponsored) **FBI, international terrorism*

Example(s):

 “*the fight against terrorism"*

“The British Columbia prosecution service said that Lo could face more charges, but investigators ruled out terrorism and said Lo had a history of mental health issues.:”

*—Mark Weinstein, MSNBC Newsweek, 28 Apr. 2025*

“If a cyberattack is to blame for the loss of power, Judge Jorge Calama would then investigate the case as a potential act of terrorism, according to court documents.”

*—Julia Gomez, USA Today, 29 Apr. 2025*

## torment

tormented; tormenting; torments

tor·​ment, TOR-ment,  *transistive verb*

: to cause severe, usually persistent or recurrent distress of the body or mind

Examples: 

“The cattle were tormented by flies.”

“He was tormented by doubt.”

“She liked me, but she laughed at me, and tormented me…” *- Charles Dickens*

*—Merriam Webster Dictionary*

## transparent brain-computer interfaces (BCIs)

: Transparent Brain-Computer Interfaces (BCIs) utilize nanotechnology to create devices that are both functional and transparent, allowing for observation of the underlying brain tissue during operation. This transparency is crucial for research and may offer advantages in future clinical applications.

*—*[*Psychology Today*](https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/the-future-brain/202401/transparent-brain-computer-interface-uses-ai-and-nanotech)*,* [*Google AI Overview*](https://www.google.com/search?q=bci+definition&oq=bci+definition&gs_lcrp=EgZjaHJvbWUyBggAEEUYOTIHCAEQABiABDIHCAIQABiABDINCAMQLhivARjHARiABDIKCAQQABixAxiABDIHCAUQABiABDIHCAYQABiABDINCAcQLhjHARjRAxiABDIHCAgQABiABDIHCAkQABiABNIBCDQzMTNqMGo3qAIAsAIA&sourceid=chrome&ie=UTF-8)

# assistance

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


Help given to a person: support or aid. Merriam-Webster defines to assist as to “give usually supplementary support or aid to” someone. In health care, social services and everyday life, assistance means things like care and treatment, financial support, a safe place to stay, and an explanation of what is happening.

Why it matters here: statements recorded in the journal ask, again and again, “Do you accept assistance?” while what they offer is euthanization. Ending a person's life is not assistance in the ordinary meaning of the word. Where the law allows medical aid in dying, as Colorado does, it is a narrow medical process that a person with a terminal illness must ask for themselves; it is never offered by a voice or a stranger, and nobody can be held until they agree to it.

Related terms: [euthanasia outside legitimate health care](/glossary/euthanasia-outside-health-care), [self-elected suicide](/glossary/self-elected-suicide), [military ugly workflow](/glossary/military-ugly-workflow)

Sources: [assist — Merriam-Webster](https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/assist) · [What does safe sharing look like?](/concepts/safe-sharing)

# atrocities (atrocity crimes)

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


The United Nations' umbrella term for the three most serious international crimes: genocide, crimes against humanity and war crimes. Ethnic cleansing is grouped with them, though it is not a separate crime in law. The UN's 2014 Framework of Analysis for Atrocity Crimes sets out the warning signs for preventing them.

Documented cases: the 1994 genocide in Rwanda, where the 1998 judgment against a Rwandan official was the first genocide conviction by an international court; Srebrenica in July 1995, ruled a genocide on appeal in 2004, with the Bosnian Serb army commander's life sentence upheld in 2021; and Cambodia, where the Khmer Rouge tribunal convicted surviving leaders of genocide in 2018.

Related terms: [crimes against humanity](/glossary/crimes-against-humanity), [war crimes](/glossary/war-crimes), [International Criminal Court](/glossary/international-criminal-court), [tribunal](/glossary/tribunal)

Sources: [Definitions — UN Office on Genocide Prevention](https://www.un.org/en/genocide-prevention/definition) · [Framework of Analysis for Atrocity Crimes — OHCHR](https://www.ohchr.org/en/documents/tools-and-resources/framework-analysis-atrocity-crimes) · [Rwanda: the first conviction for genocide — USHMM](https://encyclopedia.ushmm.org/content/en/article/rwanda-the-first-conviction-for-genocide) · [Krstić case information sheet — ICTY](https://www.icty.org/x/cases/krstic/cis/en/cis_krstic_en.pdf) · [Mladić — International Residual Mechanism for Criminal Tribunals](https://www.irmct.org/en/cases/mict-13-56) · [The Khmer Rouge cases — USHMM](https://www.ushmm.org/genocide-prevention/countries/cambodia/the-cases)

# blood–brain barrier (BBB)

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


A tightly sealed lining of the blood vessels in the brain that keeps most substances in the blood from reaching brain tissue. It protects the brain from toxins and infection, and it also blocks most medicines.

Getting particles across it is one of the main goals, and main obstacles, of nanotechnology aimed at the brain. Researchers have tested particles that cross it with the help of a magnet or focused ultrasound in animals. A 2019 proposal for future brain-linked nanorobots assumed they would cross it; nothing of that kind has been built.

Related terms: [nanoparticle](/glossary/nanoparticle), [nanomedicine](/glossary/nanomedicine), [magnetoelectric nanoparticles](/glossary/magnetoelectric-nanoparticles), [neuralnanorobotics](/glossary/neuralnanorobotics)

Sources: [Blood–brain barrier — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood%E2%80%93brain_barrier); [Human Brain/Cloud Interface — Frontiers in Neuroscience (2019)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6450227/)

# Breaching

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


BREE·ching

*See [Diving](/glossary/diving) — the same process under its later name.*

The earlier term used in this work for gaining access to a person's cognition: establishing a brain-to-brain connection for the purpose of observation or investigation. Previous versions of this glossary drew a distinction between the two — breaching as the initial entry, diving as what followed. The author has since confirmed they are the same thing, and that breaching was simply the word he used first.

This entry is kept rather than merged away because the record depends on it: *breaching* is what 125 journal entries actually say, and links to this page should not break.

Related terms: [Diving](/glossary/diving), [Neuro-engagement](/glossary/neuro-engagement)

# calcium imaging

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


A way of seeing neurons fire. When a neuron is active, calcium floods into it. Researchers add a fluorescent marker that glows when calcium is present, so a microscope can record which cells are active and when.

It shows activity cell by cell, which electrodes on the surface of the brain cannot. It needs an optical view of the tissue and, in most cases, genetically modified animals.

Documented real-world example: in the UC San Diego study behind [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis), deep calcium activity recorded with [two-photon microscopy](/glossary/two-photon-microscopy) served as the answer key. An AI model learned to predict it from electrical signals on the brain’s surface alone.

Related terms: [two-photon microscopy](/glossary/two-photon-microscopy), [neural decoding](/glossary/neural-decoding), [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis)

Sources: [Calcium imaging — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcium_imaging); [Transparent Brain-Computer Interface Uses AI and Nanotech — Psychology Today (Cami Rosso, 24 Jan 2024)](https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/the-future-brain/202401/transparent-brain-computer-interface-uses-ai-and-nanotech)

# Colorado Privacy Act

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


A Colorado consumer-privacy law (SB21-190), signed on 7 July 2021 and in force since 1 July 2023. Businesses must get a person's opt-in consent before processing “sensitive data”.

An amendment, HB24-1058, in force since 7 August 2024, added biological data to that category, including neural data: measured activity of a person's nervous system. Only the Colorado Attorney General and district attorneys can enforce the Act.

Related terms: [cognitive liberty](/glossary/cognitive-liberty), [neuro-forensics](/glossary/neuro-forensics), [brain–computer interface](/glossary/braincomputer-interface-bci)

Sources: [Colorado Privacy Act — Colorado Attorney General](https://coag.gov/resources/colorado-privacy-act/) · [HB24-1058 — Colorado General Assembly](https://leg.colorado.gov/bills/hb24-1058)

# contact microphone

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


also “piezo pickup”, “contact pickup”

A microphone that hears solid objects instead of air. A piezoelectric element is pressed or bonded against a surface and responds to the surface’s vibration, which makes it almost completely insensitive to sound arriving through the room. Guitarists use them on instrument bodies. They cost around ten to fifteen dollars.

Their value here is diagnostic. If a window or wall is being driven as a loudspeaker — see [structure-borne audio](/glossary/structure-borne-audio) — a contact microphone on that surface registers it directly, while an ordinary air microphone in the same room hears only room tone. The instrument distinguishes between sound arriving through the air and sound being produced by the structure itself.

Documented real-world example: the counter-surveillance literature treats a windowpane as an acoustic component in both directions. A 2016 paper in the Journal of Vibroengineering, proposing a defence against laser eavesdropping, specifies a piezoceramic transducer bonded to the pane and driven at its first resonant mode — the same physics, used deliberately.

One honest limit: vendor literature on technical surveillance countermeasures lists contact microphones as a THREAT to be defended against, not as standard sweep equipment. No government or standards-body manual was found prescribing them as diagnostic instruments. Useful, cheap and physically correct for the question — but not, on the available evidence, established professional practice.

Related terms: [structure-borne audio](/glossary/structure-borne-audio), [piezoelectricity](/glossary/piezoelectricity), [laser microphone](/glossary/laser-microphone), [sampling limit](/glossary/sampling-limit)

Sources: [Contact microphone — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_microphone); [Protection of speech information against laser eavesdropping — Journal of Vibroengineering (2016)](https://www.extrica.com/article/16943); [TSCM threats, detection equipment, practices and procedures — Bastille](https://bastille.net/centers-of-excellence/tscm-threats-detection-equipment-practices-procedures/)

# crimes against humanity

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


Acts such as murder, torture or persecution committed as part of a widespread or systematic attack on a civilian population, defined in Article 7 of the Rome Statute. Unlike war crimes, they can happen in peacetime. Random or isolated acts do not qualify.

Documented cases: the Nuremberg tribunal's 1946 judgment established crimes against humanity as crimes under international law, and 19 defendants were convicted. In the 1947 Doctors' Trial, 16 German physicians and administrators were convicted, including for the Nazi “euthanasia” programme, and 7 were sentenced to death. In 2014 the Khmer Rouge tribunal convicted two senior leaders for the forced emptying of Cambodia's cities. In 2021 the International Criminal Court convicted a Ugandan commander on 61 counts of crimes against humanity and war crimes, with a sentence of 25 years.

Related terms: [war crimes](/glossary/war-crimes), [atrocities](/glossary/atrocities), [Nuremberg Code](/glossary/nuremberg-code), [International Criminal Court](/glossary/international-criminal-court)

Sources: [Definitions — UN Office on Genocide Prevention](https://www.un.org/en/genocide-prevention/definition) · [International Military Tribunal at Nuremberg — USHMM](https://encyclopedia.ushmm.org/content/en/article/international-military-tribunal-at-nuremberg) · [The Doctors Trial — USHMM](https://encyclopedia.ushmm.org/content/en/article/the-doctors-trial-the-medical-case-of-the-subsequent-nuremberg-proceedings) · [The Khmer Rouge cases — USHMM](https://www.ushmm.org/genocide-prevention/countries/cambodia/the-cases) · [ICC sentences Ongwen — UN News](https://news.un.org/en/story/2021/05/1091472)

# Diving

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


DY·ving

*also called breaching*

A military / telepathic term for establishing a brain-to-brain connection with another human being or animal for the purpose of observation and/or investigation via image-based search.

**Breaching is the earlier name for the same process.** The author used that word before learning the term Diving, and it is the word the record itself uses: *breaching* appears 271 times across 125 journal entries, while *diving* appears in none of them. A reader working through the journal will meet the older word first, and should read the two as one thing.

Related terms: [Breaching](/glossary/breaching), [image-based search](/glossary/image-based-search), [Neuro-engagement](/glossary/neuro-engagement), [telepathy](/glossary/telepathy)

# dumping syndrome

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


rapid gastric emptying

A group of symptoms caused by food moving too quickly from the stomach into the small intestine: diarrhea, nausea, and feeling light-headed or tired after a meal.

Early dumping begins within 30 minutes of eating; late dumping comes one to three hours after. The most common cause is surgery on the stomach or esophagus.

Sources: [Dumping Syndrome — National Institute of Diabetes and Digestive and Kidney Diseases (NIH)](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/digestive-diseases/dumping-syndrome)

# electrocorticography (ECoG)

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


ee·LEK·troh·kor·tih·KOG·ruh·fee

Recording electrical activity directly from the surface of the brain, using a thin sheet of electrodes placed under the skull. It picks up clearer signals than an [electroencephalogram](/glossary/electroencephalogram) on the scalp, but it requires surgery.

It is used in hospitals to locate the source of seizures before epilepsy surgery, and in research brain–computer interfaces. Smaller, denser versions are often called micro-ECoG.

Documented real-world example: the [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis) built at UC San Diego are a high-density, transparent micro-ECoG array made of [graphene](/glossary/graphene).

Related terms: [neural interface](/glossary/neural-interface), [electroencephalogram](/glossary/electroencephalogram), [graphene](/glossary/graphene), [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis), [hardware](/glossary/hardware-computer)

# electromagnetic field (EMF)

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


A physical field produced by electrically charged objects and by the flow of electric current — the combination of an electric field (created by voltage) and a magnetic field (created by moving current). When these fields change, they propagate through space as electromagnetic waves, spanning the spectrum from power-line frequencies through radio and microwaves to infrared, visible light, and beyond. Every electrical and electronic device produces electromagnetic fields, and they are the medium through which all wireless signals travel. Most everyday EMFs — from power lines, phones, and Wi-Fi — are non-ionizing.

Related terms: [non-ionizing radiation](/glossary/non-ionizing-radiation), [Directed-energy](/glossary/directed-energy), [microwave auditory effect](/glossary/microwave-auditory-effect), [hardware](/glossary/hardware-computer), [magnetoelectric nanoparticles](/glossary/magnetoelectric-nanoparticles)

Sources: [Electromagnetic fields — World Health Organization](https://www.who.int/news-room/questions-and-answers/item/radiation-electromagnetic-fields); [Electromagnetic field — Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/science/electromagnetic-field)

# energy harvesting

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


Capturing small amounts of ambient energy — vibration, movement, heat, light — and converting it into usable electricity. A genuine engineering discipline, and the umbrella term covering piezoelectric, photovoltaic and triboelectric devices.

It is also among the most oversold ideas in urban infrastructure. No city anywhere has adopted electricity-generating pavement as working infrastructure. Every installation on record is an event demonstration, a grant-funded pilot, a vendor showcase, or a failure that was removed.

Documented real-world example: the California Energy Commission commissioned an independent assessment of piezoelectric roadway harvesting (CEC-500-2013-007, 2014). It measured system output at 44 to 440 watts per kilometre of road, against vendor claims of more than 100 kilowatts per kilometre — a gap of two to three orders of magnitude. Washington DC installed 194 kinetic pavers at Dupont Circle in November 2016 for roughly $300,000 and has never published an evaluation. Sandpoint, Idaho’s solar roadway averaged 0.25 kWh a day in its best month, about one sixth of a single rooftop panel, and shut down in December 2018. A €5m solar road at Tourouvre-au-Perche in France saw annual output fall from 150,000 to 38,000 kWh before sections were destroyed and removed. A solar expressway in Jinan, China opened on 28 December 2017 and closed within days after panels shattered under traffic.

A note on vocabulary, because the marketing and the engineering use different words. Energy harvesting, photovoltaic pavement and triboelectric nanogenerator are engineering terms. Kinetic pavement, smart street and solar roadway are trade names. Pavegen’s tiles, frequently described as piezoelectric, in fact work by electromagnetic induction. And footstep harvesting is parasitic: the electricity comes from the extra work the pedestrian does compressing the tile.

Related terms: [piezoelectricity](/glossary/piezoelectricity)

Sources: [Assessment of Piezoelectric Materials for Roadway Energy Harvesting, CEC-500-2013-007 — California Energy Commission](https://pop.h-cdn.co/assets/cm/15/06/54d152f0cee9f_-_CEC-500-2013-007.pdf); [This Dupont Circle sidewalk turns footsteps into power — Washington Post](https://www.washingtonpost.com/local/trafficandcommuting/this-dupont-circle-sidewalk-turns-footsteps-into-power/2016/11/30/c69263f8-b020-11e6-8616-52b15787add0_story.html); [Solar Roadways — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_Roadways); [China’s solar highway was a victim of heavy traffic and bad design — South China Morning Post](https://www.scmp.com/news/china/society/article/2131241/chinas-solar-highway-was-victim-heavy-traffic-and-bad-design-not)

# euthanasia outside legitimate health care

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


Ending a person's life outside the narrow legal process for assisted dying. It is illegal in every country, including the countries and states that allow assisted dying, and it is prosecuted as murder or manslaughter.

Why: where assisted dying is legal, as in Canada, the Netherlands, Belgium, and US states such as Oregon and Colorado, it is allowed only under strict medical law. The person must ask for it themselves, freely and more than once, while able to make the decision; they must meet medical criteria such as a terminal or serious incurable illness; independent clinicians must confirm this; and each case is recorded and reviewed. No law anywhere allows someone to decide that another person should die, to pressure or persuade them toward death, or to carry it out outside that process.

The right to life is protected in international human rights law (Article 6 of the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights). After the Second World War, people who carried out the Nazi "euthanasia" program, which killed people with disabilities without their consent, were tried for murder and crimes against humanity.

Related terms: [self-elected suicide](/glossary/self-elected-suicide), [Nuremberg Code](/glossary/nuremberg-code), [crimes against humanity](/glossary/crimes-against-humanity), [euthanasia](/glossary/euthanisia)

Sources: [International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights — OHCHR](https://www.ohchr.org/en/instruments-mechanisms/instruments/international-covenant-civil-and-political-rights) · [The "euthanasia" program — US Holocaust Memorial Museum](https://encyclopedia.ushmm.org/content/en/article/euthanasia-program)

# false disclosure

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


also “false-flag” disclosure

A hypothesis advanced by Dr. Steven Greer — physician and founder of the Disclosure Project — that elements of the military-industrial complex are preparing a staged, deceptive “disclosure” of extraterrestrial life: using human-built craft, holographic or otherwise simulated phenomena, and staged events to portray extraterrestrials as a hostile threat. Greer argues the purpose would be to justify a vast military and space-weapons buildup and a consolidation of power — a “false-flag” alien threat — as opposed to what he calls authentic disclosure. Presented here as Greer's hypothesis, not as established fact.

Related terms: [perceptual set](/glossary/perceptual-set), [cognitive liberty](/glossary/cognitive-liberty), [Directed-energy](/glossary/directed-energy)

Sources: [Update on False UFO Disclosure — Steven Greer (Disclosure News)](https://www.disclosurenews.it/update-on-false-ufo-disclosure-steven-greer/); [Are Secret Space Program Disclosures Prelude to a False-Flag Alien Invasion? — Exopolitics](https://exopolitics.org/are-secret-space-program-disclosures-prelude-to-a-false-flag-alien-invasion/)

# Gang stalking

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


gang STAWK·ing

A vernacular term — originating within the targeted-individual community — for the coordinated surveillance and harassment of a single individual by multiple people acting in concert. It names the reported pattern of coordinated harassment, as distinct from a specific documented methodology such as Zersetzung tactics. Most often reported by those who identify as targeted individuals.

Documented real-world example: In 2019, executives and employees of eBay orchestrated a cyberstalking campaign against David and Ina Steiner, a Massachusetts couple who published a newsletter critical of the company — sending disturbing packages (a bloody pig mask, a fetal pig, a funeral wreath, live insects), posting anonymous threats, and traveling to Natick to surveil them and place a GPS tracker on their car. Seven eBay employees were federally charged; several were convicted and imprisoned, and eBay entered a deferred-prosecution agreement and paid a $3 million criminal penalty. The company later settled the Steiners' civil suit for $55.7 million (2026).

Related terms: [Targeted individual](/glossary/targeted-individual), [Zersetzung tactics](/glossary/zersetzung-tactics), [No-touch torture](/glossary/no-touch-torture)

Sources: [United States v. eBay Inc. — U.S. DOJ](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ma/case/us-v-ebay-inc); [$55.7M settlement — Law.com](https://www.law.com/2026/07/28/ebay-former-execs-settle-mass-couples-harassment-cyberstalking-suit-for-557m/)

# graphene

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


A single layer of carbon atoms arranged in a honeycomb pattern. It is one atom thick, very strong, flexible, conducts electricity well, and lets almost all light through.

Those properties are why researchers use it for brain electrodes. A graphene array can record electrical signals from the surface of the brain while a microscope looks straight through it at the tissue beneath. That is the idea behind [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis).

Graphene was first isolated in 2004. Andre Geim and Konstantin Novoselov received the 2010 Nobel Prize in Physics for it.

Graphene oxide is a different, chemically altered form, studied separately for uses such as drug delivery. The two names are often confused.

Related terms: [nanomaterial](/glossary/nanomaterial), [electrocorticography](/glossary/electrocorticography), [transparent brain-computer interfaces](/glossary/transparent-brain-computer-interfaces-bcis), [lipid nanoparticle](/glossary/lipid-nanoparticle)

Sources: [The Nobel Prize in Physics 2010 — NobelPrize.org](https://www.nobelprize.org/prizes/physics/2010/summary/); [Transparent Brain-Computer Interface Uses AI and Nanotech — Psychology Today (Cami Rosso, 24 Jan 2024)](https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/the-future-brain/202401/transparent-brain-computer-interface-uses-ai-and-nanotech)

# independent investigation

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


An inquiry carried out by people who are not under the control of those being investigated, with the power to obtain records, question witnesses and publish what they find. In the United States these have included congressional committees, inspectors general, presidential commissions and the courts; internationally, UN special rapporteurs and commissions of inquiry.

Precedent shows why they matter. In 1975 the Rockefeller Commission and the Senate's Church Committee brought to light MKUltra, a CIA program (1953–1973) that tested drugs, hypnosis and other methods of influencing the mind on people, some of whom never knew. Most of its records had been destroyed in 1973, and a 1977 Senate hearing uncovered more. More recently, after US diplomats and officers reported unexplained sounds, pressure and injuries from 2016 onward ("Havana syndrome"), Congress passed the HAVANA Act in 2021 to support them, and US agencies have reviewed the cases; their conclusions remain disputed.

Related terms: [obedience experiment](/glossary/obedience-experiment), [Nuremberg Code](/glossary/nuremberg-code), [tribunal](/glossary/tribunal)

Sources: [The Church Committee — US Senate](https://www.senate.gov/about/powers-procedures/investigations/church-committee.htm) · [HAVANA Act of 2021 — Congress.gov](https://www.congress.gov/bill/117th-congress/senate-bill/1828)

# injectable mesh electronics

*Site-authored. This term was written for invisibleships.com to explain the work; it is not extracted from the source document series, and it carries no source-document id for that reason. Terms drawn from the primary record are in `glossary/` and do carry one.*


also “neural lace”

A flexible, net-like electronic mesh built from nanowires, thin and soft enough to be drawn into a syringe and injected into the brain, where it unfolds and records neural activity. The mesh is about 90 per cent open space, like chicken wire, and is far more flexible than earlier implants, so brain tissue grows around and through it.

It was demonstrated in mice by a Harvard research group and published in Nature Nanotechnology in 2015. In that work, the mesh connected by wires to recording equipment outside the head; it was not wireless.

“Neural lace” is a science-fiction term (from Iain M. Banks’s novels) that was later applied to this kind of work.

Related terms: [neural interface](/glossary/neural-interface), [nanomaterial](/glossary/nanomaterial), [hardware](/glossary/hardware-computer), [neural dust](/glossary/neural-dust)

Sources: [Syringe-injectable electronics — Liu et al., Nature Nanotechnology (2015)](https://collaborate.princeton.edu/en/publications/syringe-injectable-electronics/); [Injecting electronics into brain not as freaky as it sounds — National Geographic (2015)](https://www.nationalgeographic.com/science/article/injecting-electronics-into-brain-not-as-freaky-as-it-sounds)
